                                                                                                                                       Editor: THINK FOR YOURSELF, SCHMUCK. PO Bo• 2255 1 , Memphis. TN 38 1 22·0551
                                                                                                         Dear DHARMA COMBAT,
                                                                                                                Crowbar's right when he speaks of your mag taking some getting into but repaying the
                                                                                                         effort. The format tends to bristle, i t's not what they call •a quick study•, but on the whole
                                                                                                         the parts that I've read so tar (the Dylan commentary, Bob Black's article, the reviews, etc.)
                                                                                                         have been interesting and in several Instances even provocative.
                                                                                                                As regards Bob Dylan - I sat down the other night and watched DON'T LOOK BACK again
                                                                                                         after, oh 1 guess it must be nearly fifteen years since I'd last seen it. The film contains a
                                                                                                         good deal of posturing that looks rather fudgelike and jejune now but quite a lot of it still holds
                                                                                                         up, particularly the almost hair-raisingly spooky moment when Dylan suddenly bursts into
                                                                                                         song while Donovan looks on. But to watch the thing is to realize what a frail figure Dylan
                                                                                                         was  -·   like a little piece of porcelain moving through some ponderous hoary furnace      •• a very
                                                                                                          strong sense that, more than most, he was making it all up as he went along, and you have to
                                                                                                          wonder, or anyway 1 had to wonder, where on eanh in his room back in Minnesota did he find
                                                                                                          enough time (or materials) to Invent something like Bob Oylan? Of all the 60's rock stars,
                                                                                                          Dylan seems to be the most completely self-invented. Watching DON'T LOOK BACK is a good
                                                                                                          reminder of how alone he was. Very strong, almost eerie sense that he probably had no
                                                                                                          friends at all ••     certainly no one around him to count on or to confide In, etc. His own
                                                                                                          solitariness surrounds him like a kind of nimbus. Inky.
                                                                                                                 Also, a nice sequence when he compares himself to Caruso -- •1 can sing as good as
                                                                                                          Caruso; something like that: and I found myself thinking, yes, you can ; sure, even more than
                                                                                                          the lyrics it's the sound that carries Bob Dylan. I wonder, was it the motorcycle accident, as
                                                                                                          your reviewer says, that knocked him for a loop? or was it simply the pressure , the
                                                                                                          repetition of being called on again and again so many limes to give reprises of his own stuff
                                                                                                          that perhaps deafened him to his own music? For me, it starts getting a little sour around the
                                                                                                          time that he •finds religion.· Could it be the old familiar trap at work       when the content, In
                                                                                                                                                                                        -·


                                                                                                          this case the religious content, starts to become overt, and insisted on, then the vessel
                                                                                                          ceases to embody it? Well, hell if I know.
                                                                                                                 Could it be that as lime went along he forgot or lost the knack of being able to see what a
                                                                                                          tremendous joke it all was ... too much coffee can do that to a guy.
                                                                                                                 This. of course, Is something that can happen to even the best of our saints and avatars.
                                                                                                          But when the going Is good    ••    then the laughter Is generally there, even when it's taking care
                                                                                                          to conceal itself In mock-seriousness. Buddha laughed when he beheld the Infinitude of
                                                                                                          compounded universes. THE MAHABHARATA Is the longest shaggy-dog story In the world. In
                                                                                                          THE ACTS OF JOHN, that mixed-gnostic chestnut, Jesus says, ·And if you would understand
                                                                                                          what I am, know this: all that I have said I have uttered playfully, and I was no means
                                                                                                          ashamed thereby: Just so ...
                                                                                                                 The other day while I was standing on a chair to innocently peek across the yard at my
                                                                                                          nelghbor's wife. 1 had a sudden Illumination that enlightened me perfectly as to what all this
                                                                                                          means in terms of JOB VI, 1 �.          That passage••   ·For the fainting
                                                                                                                                                                                   ••  from his friend-loyalty•
                                                                                                         ••   which has kept rationalist commentators arguing for so many ages, now. Suddenly, In that
                                                                                                          one moment while I was watching the lady next door, it became crystal-clear to me , the
                                                                                                          answer, the secret key to the unsolvable problem. But then I became so absorbed In watching
                                                                                                           that I forgot what it was.

                                                                                                               DR. AL ACKERMAN

                   And Mysticism                                                                        T he social machines that were these early civilizations eventually (d) evolved in to
                                                                                                        megamachines, social structures capable of stupendous feats of creation and destruction on �
                                                                                                        ever-Increasing scale. We may never know wt'ly this situation came to be, but it Is certaa:'
                                                                                                        that this structure became dominant in the minds and bodies of more and more �pies, and il
                                                                                                        remains Intact today. The psychological functions underpinning later religious Institutions
                                                                                                        were themselves the products of just such a warped and convoluted worldview, one In which
                                                                                                        one 'progresses' by stepping on the beings surrounding one, be they animals, humans, or the
                                                                                                        earth Itself.


                                                                                                        A major alteration In the megamachlne occurred In Greek kulture. The Greeks altered the
                                                                                                        power dynamics of Institutionalized religion and the state. A shiH In the power structure
                                                                                                        occurred, which was Intrinsically Interconnected with a change In he psychic relationships
                                                                                                        held by people, a change In their basic anltudes toward the world and toward each other. �
                                                                                                        how they view and experienced what they considered to be reality. This was aided by lheit
                                                                                                        perfection of the art of lying, which cloaked their power-driven assault on life with skillfut
                                                                                                        rhetoric. Even though they maintained a veneer of religious devotion (which later kultures
                                                                                                        were able to dispense with) the real exploitative power of the priesthood and the devotion
                                                                                                        which lt commanded was transferred to the state, both In the language of the philosophy and
                                                                                                        In the commands of the banlefields. This power play was obviously not complete, but it began
                                                                                                        a process which paved the way for the development of our current attitudes about the wond.
                                                                                                        attitudes which underlie our economic, soc:Jal, and psychological conditioning.

                                                                                                              Some scholars attributed to Greece the breaking with the archetypal �cllc world �.
                                                                                                              introducing the objective mode of thinking le�ding to science. B� t...thc Creeks dest � yed
                                                                                                              In the resulting Creek represent�tion of reality, humans are passave and helpless bus of
                                                                                                              protoplasm aught in the grindstones of f�ted c:osrnk forces.2



                                                                                                        The Inquisition and the witch-hunts which took place during the so-called Middle Ages further
                                                                                                        consolidated the power of the religious functions over the minds of Europeans. Even though
                                                                                                        the religious Institutions themselves lost much power as political entities, the world-views
                                                                                                        and psycholog ical relationships underlying them did not. They were merely transferred _,
                                                                                                        secular Institutions, and the witch-hunt model of exploitation and oppression can be seen
                                                                                                        throughout the ages (no matter how much or how little disguised) In many examples, such as
                                                                                                        In Nazi Germany, U.S. foreign policy (sic), and our current laws concerning abortion rights.
                                                                                                        This shift in power which was begun by the Greeks, was renewed in force by the
                                                                                                        · Renaissance·. Through the image and the practice of the Renaissance man, the dynamic
                                                                                                        forces of individualism, creativity, invention, and genius were placed securely in the service
                                                                                                        of the state. The Renaissance artists devoted themselves to their patrons and the state
                                                                                                        machinery with such abandon lhat they pushed the limits of human possibility In many
                                                                                                        directions.3 The •greatness· they achieved served to glorify the institutions of power,
                                                                                                        binding the function of the individ� al to the function of the state.
